Even in unconstrained settings, Hexels offers complementary quality-memory trade-offs. For efficient 3D surface reconstruction, Hexels reduce error by 28.9% compared with memory-matched 2DGS across 15 DTU scans. On 6 Tanks and Temples surface scenes, Hexels achieves the highest mean F1 among compared methods (34.38%, versus 31.72% for 2DGS). We further also show that Hexels, as a general primitive, improves 2D image representation over Image-GS. Hexels push the Pareto frontier on the quality-efficiency trade-off of surface-aligned primitives.</p></details> | Project page at https://honeycomb-surfels.github.io/ |

Project page: https://nianticspatial.github.io/groundcontrol</p></details> | Accepted to WACV 2027. Project page: https://nianticspatial.github.io/groundcontrol |

| **[CTBakeGS: training-free baking of photorealistic CT volume renderings into 3D Gaussian splats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37406v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Photorealistic volume rendering of computed tomography (CT) may convey anatomy intuitively, but it needs the CT data, dedicated software and a powerful graphics processor. Their colour and opacity were computed on the graphics processor with the renderer's lighting, cell opacity was converted analytically into splat opacity, and faint splats were retained stochastically above the 1/255 opacity floor of standard 3DGS rasterisers. Two-million-splat models reached a PSNR of 32.6 +/- 0.9 dB for bone, 30.1 +/- 1.0 dB for contrast-enhanced vessels and 32.3 +/- 1.5 dB for skin and bone. A conversion pass (usually two or three per model) took 4-30 s on a current laptop and 9-60 s on a desktop with a 4-GB graphics card. On the laptop, the models rendered at 68 frames per second at 800 x 800 pixels. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |

| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. | **[CTBakeGS: training-free baking of photorealistic CT volume renderings into 3D Gaussian splats](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37406v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Photorealistic volume rendering of computed tomography (CT) may convey anatomy intuitively, but it needs the CT data, dedicated software and a powerful graphics processor. Three-dimensional Gaussian splatting (3DGS) displays photorealistic scenes in real time on less powerful devices, but existing medical approaches train each scene for minutes to hours. We aimed to convert a CT volume rendering directly into a 3DGS model without training. Splats were placed on an adaptive octree of millimetre-sized cells. Their colour and opacity were computed on the graphics processor with the renderer's lighting, cell opacity was converted analytically into splat opacity, and faint splats were retained stochastically above the 1/255 opacity floor of standard 3DGS rasterisers. Six public CT scans (1.5-mm voxels) were converted with three transfer functions and compared with converged references by peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), structural similarity and a learned perceptual metric. Two-million-splat models reached a PSNR of 32.6 +/- 0.9 dB for bone, 30.1 +/- 1.0 dB for contrast-enhanced vessels and 32.3 +/- 1.5 dB for skin and bone. A conversion pass (usually two or three per model) took 4-30 s on a current laptop and 9-60 s on a desktop with a 4-GB graphics card. On the laptop, the models rendered at 68 frames per second at 800 x 800 pixels. Training-free conversion produced real-time 3DGS models of photorealistic CT renderings in about a minute or less per conversion pass on ordinary computers, with fidelity that we consider sufficient for interactive viewing.</p></details> | Code and project page: https://github.com/BocchiSlice/CTBakeGS |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[DomeGS: Perceptually Consistent Background Completion for Unbounded 3D Gaussian Splatting in Virtual Reality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37172v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The growing need for high-quality 3D content remains among the most prevalent challenges in virtual reality (VR). However, these approaches struggle to blend seamlessly with the reconstructed 3DGS geometry. To address these issues, we introduce the DomeGS framework, which incorporates the skydome concept not as a flat background shell, but as a part of the reconstructed Gaussian Splatting 3D scene. By integrating the horizon and sky into the 3DGS representation, our method provides a unified, scene-consistent environment bridging foregrounds and backgrounds for greater realism. Experimental evaluation with N=36 users demonstrates that DomeGS significantly increases spatial presence (p=0.042) and realism (p=0.042) compared to the skybox baseline.</p></details> | 9 pages, 10 figues |
| **[LoBE-GS: Load-Balanced and Efficient 3D Gaussian Splatting for Large-Scale Scene Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.01767v3)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has established itself as an efficient representation for real-time, high-fidelity 3D scene reconstruction. To accelerate preprocessing, it employs depth-based back-projection for fast camera assignment, reducing processing time from hours to minutes. It further reduces training cost through two lightweight techniques: visibility cropping and selective densification. On large-scale urban and outdoor datasets, LoBE-GS reduces scene-partitioning time by up to an order of magnitude and worst-block fine-training time by up to $3.2\times$ relative to CityGaussian. Existing methods deform Gaussian splats through direct linear blend skinning (LBS) or mesh proxies, both of which are prone to joint-boundary artifacts under articulation, even with per-primitive corrections. Under LBS each splat can be moved, rotated, or rescaled but never bent, so the canonical tiling comes apart at joint boundaries. Proximity attention point rendering (PAPR) instead carries no per-primitive shape; each pixel is recomposed at render time from the deformed primitives' positions, so the surface re-forms naturally with the articulation. We present RigPAPR, which auto-rigs a static PAPR cloud and drives it under direct LBS from a single fixed-viewpoint video, without a category template or a pose-dependent correction network. RigPAPR is competitive with the strongest baseline at the supervised view and outperforms splatting-based and mesh-based baselines at novel views, by 3~dB PSNR on synthetic subjects and about 2~dB on real-world subjects, with cleaner joint-boundary renderings.</p></details> | Project page is available at https://niopeng.github.io/RigPAPR/ |
| **[RecallGS: Geometry-Grounded Recall for Online Semantic Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31293v1)** | 2026-10-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Online semantic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables open-vocabulary scene understanding from streaming observations. However, existing methods recover geometric context missing from image-derived semantic features using an additionally learned 3D network, incurring extra training and runtime overhead. We observe that such geometric-semantic evidence may already exist in the agent's observation history and can be retrieved rather than learned. Based on this insight, we introduce RecallGS (Geometry-Grounded Recall for Online Semantic Gaussian Splatting), a lightweight, training-free mechanism that treats past observations as a non-parametric geometric-semantic prior. RecallGS follows a recall-ground-fuse pipeline: CLIP retrieves relevant observations, alpha-compositing weights identify geometrically supported views, and aggregate their semantic features over each Gaussian's rendered footprint. On ScanNet and ScanNet++, RecallGS achieves strong semantic segmentation performance at 2.48 FPS, demonstrating that geometric-semantic context can be effectively recovered from observation history without an additional learned 3D branch.</p></details> |  |
| **[No Pose, No Problem in 4D: Feed-Forward Dynamic Gaussians from Unposed Multi-View Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22190v2)** | 2026-10-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent feed-forward 3D gaussian splatting methods have made dramatic progress on individual aspects of 3D scene reconstruction, but no existing method jointly addresses dynamic content, multi-view input, and unknown camera poses in a single feed-forward pass. Methods that handle dynamics either require accurate camera poses or accept only monocular input; pose-free multi-view methods address only static scenes; and per-scene optimization methods bridge some of these gaps but at minutes-to-hours cost per scene. We introduce NoPo4D, the first feed-forward system that addresses this empty quadrant. Building on a pretrained geometry backbone and recent 4D Gaussian frameworks, NoPo4D introduces a velocity decomposition that splits Gaussian motion into per-pixel image-plane shifts and depth changes, allowing direct supervision from pseudo ground-truth optical flow on the 2D component. This sidesteps both the differentiable rendering that couples prior posed methods to pose accuracy and the 3D motion ground truth that prior pose-free methods require. The system is rounded out by a bidirectional motion encoder for cross-view and cross-frame feature aggregation, and view-dependent opacity that mitigates cross-view and cross-timestep Gaussian misalignments. On four multi-view dynamic benchmarks, NoPo4D consistently outperforms prior feed-forward baselines, and with an optional post-optimization stage surpasses per-scene optimization methods, while running orders of magnitude faster.</p></details> | https://bralani.github.io/nopo4d_html/ Camera ready version of NeurIPS-2026 |
| **[H-2DGS: Height-Field Gaussian Splatting for Sharp and Deformable Neurosurgical Surface Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30910v1)** | 2026-10-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Neurosurgical navigation commonly presents magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) as 2D slices, making it difficult to relate the anatomy in those slices to the cortical surface observed through the surgical microscope. We seek a common 3D visualization that combines a photorealistic reconstruction of the exposed cortex during tumor surgery with whole-brain MRI context and diffusion tractography pathways. This requires the reconstructed cortex to form a clear boundary around the brain anatomy rather than a diffuse volume that obscures internal structures. Conventional 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) can reproduce observed views but, under limited surgical view coverage, may distribute opacity through a thick and geometrically ambiguous band. We introduce height-field 2D Gaussian Splatting (H-2DGS), a three-stage pipeline that concentrates a robust 3DGS reconstruction, fuses its depth into a shared truncated signed distance field (TSDF) surface, and represents that surface with curved, surface-oriented Gaussian primitives. Separate height, color, and opacity bases provide local geometry and appearance, while a neighbor-transition objective coordinates overlapping patches. The explicit surface further supports postoperative alignment: MRI-derived depth deforms the reconstruction, and tracked-color supervision with alternating optimization preserves its appearance. Across five real neurosurgical cases, H-2DGS gives low multi-view depth inconsistency, a compact 7.97MB representation, and interactive rendering at 29--42ms per frame. The resulting sharp, deformable surface integrates clearly with tractography and MRI anatomy, including a postoperative resection cavity.</p></details> |  |
| **[SplatLab: Autonomous Scientific Discovery for 3DGS Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30468v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reproducing a published 3DGS paper takes weeks of expert engineering before any extension can be proposed. Prior autonomous-research systems read papers, draft hypotheses, and write code, but are limited by short fixed templates, low executability, and weak traceability, and none has been demonstrated end-to-end in a live computer-vision subfield with intense activity. SplatLab is an autonomous research system for 3D Gaussian Splatting that addresses these limitations. Given a research prompt, a target-paper URL, or no input at all, it reproduces a published baseline, proposes an extension, trains and ablates across Mip-NeRF 360 and LLFF, writes the manuscript, and binds every reported number to a logged training run. Five design choices distinguish it: (i) candidates mutate plugins from a verified-reproduction backbone matched to published baselines; (ii) a cascade-weighted Elo tournament across eight islands scores candidates on measured GPU PSNR with verbal-gradient feedback; (iii) a standing falsifier subtracts Elo on failure, integrating stress-test survival into selection; (iv) every reported number is bound to a logged training run, and the run cannot terminate while any claim is unbacked; (v) a seven-category 3DGS taxonomy wraps EVOLVE-BLOCK markers in domain-aligned scaffolds transferable across 3DGS repositories. On PSNR-targeted runs, SplatLab's manuscripts beat their reproduced targets by +0.18 to +3.78 dB, including +3.78 dB on astrophotographic nebula rendering, for which no prior 3DGS method exists. The research cycle compresses from months of graduate-student time to days of mostly autonomous compute. Code, all manuscripts, experiment ledgers, and the cross-run paper store are available on the project page.</p></details> | Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. 14 pages, 4 figures. Project page: https://seemandhar.github.io/splatlab |
| **[Learning from Automatic 3D Gaussian Splatting Edits for Online Bayesian Scene Change Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28490v2)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Online scene change detection requires integrating incoming observations to determine which regions have changed relative to a reference scene. We update change beliefs as new images arrive using generalised Bayesian evidence fusion. However, the prior probability of change and evidence models are unknown beforehand, and numerical Bayesian inference can be costly. We address these challenges by learning offline from automatic edits to a reference 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) model. These edits generate labelled training views without manual annotations. Together with unchanged reference observations, they provide supervision for learning scene-specific evidence models and estimating the effective pixel prior and response statistics. Online prediction combines current evidence with historical support, while closed-form local Bayes factors, parameterized by the learned response statistics, update an accumulated 3D Gaussian field. Object-guided refinement produces the final change masks. Our method achieves state-of-the-art accuracy on PASLCD and CCS3D, with $67.64\%$ and $75.82\%$ mIoU, respectively. On PASLCD, the complete pipeline runs at $17.2$\,FPS over complete query sequences, the fastest among the evaluated online methods. Project page: https://changruizhu96.github.io/work/lego-scd/</p></details> | 34 pages, 10 figures |

## Gaussian rasterization
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |
| **[Cryptanalysis and Repair of "Decentralized Multi-authority ABE for NC$^1$ from BDH"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29224v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present a plaintext-recovery attack on both the DBDH-based construction and the CBDH-based variant of Datta, Komargodski, and Waters (Journal of Cryptology, 2023). The attack succeeds with probability one in the claimed static security model using one corrupt authority and two users, each unauthorized even when granted the corrupt authority's attribute. The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Our version of the proof gives $ω_\mathbb{F}\leq9/4$ over every field $\mathbb{F}$. We also treat the rectangular matrix multiplication exponent $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. While its mortality impacts are well recognised, the interplay between heat, air pollution, and socioeconomic vulnerability remains insufficiently understood. We quantified the associations of extreme maximum temperatures and heatwaves with daily mortality in Catalonia (2012-2022) and evaluated how air pollutants-particularly ozone, nitrogen dioxide and particulate matter-and socioeconomic factors confound or modify this relationship. We conducted a time-series ecological analysis across 379 Basic Health Areas during summer months, linking mortality with meteorological, air pollution and socioeconomic indicators. Hierarchical Bayesian zero-inflated negative binomial models were fitted with nested spatiotemporal random effects (Matérn SPDE field evolving over years; month nested within year; unstructured ABS frailty). Among 210.860 summer deaths in the complete environmental sample, extreme heat and heatwaves were associated with higher mortality after multi-pollutant and socioeconomic adjustment (e.g., extreme heat at lag 2: RR $1.105$, 95% CrI $1.086$-$1.125$; heatwave at lag 3: RR $1.142$, 95% CrI $1.116$-$1.167$; posterior Prob $\approx 1$). Ozone concentrations $\geq 100\,μ$g/m$^3$ were positively associated with mortality, and extreme heat$\times$high ozone interactions further elevated risk. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. Adaptation strategies should therefore combine heat-health warnings with air quality management and equity-oriented interventions.</p></details> |  |
| **[SoLiD26: A First Principles Solid-Liquid Interface Dataset for Machine-learned Interatomic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28013v2)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs) enable simulations of larger atomistic systems over longer timescales than traditional ab initio methods but require large, diverse training data. Existing datasets lack comprehensive sampling of solid-liquid interfaces, which exhibit complex phenomena such as solvent layering and surface reconstruction critical for energy and catalytic materials. Here, we present SoLiD26, a curated solid-liquid interface dataset of 15.4 million first-principles structures with up to 576 atoms and 15 chemical elements for training and evaluating MLIPs. The structures were compiled from density functional theory (DFT) calculations, with most configurations originating from ab initio molecular dynamics (AIMD) simulations. SoLiD26 includes aqueous coinage metal interfaces, electrode-electrolyte systems, and selected bulk reference structures, calculated with VASP using the PBE functional and D3 dispersion corrections. The application of SoLiD26 for training and evaluating MLIPs is demonstrated with a suite of MACE models on a simple training, validation and test split. This dataset provides a valuable resource for advancing MLIP development targeted at modelling solid-liquid interfaces in electrochemical and catalytic systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[A study of the $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances with a special emphasis on $C_{ab}$ curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26649v1)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In the seminal paper [13], Farrán and Munuera introduced a method to establish generic information on the $m$th Feng-Rao distance via a function on arbitrary numerical semigroups that serves as a lower bound on the generalized Hamming weights of dual one-point algebraic geometry codes whenever the semigroup is Weierstrass. In the present paper, we study what we shall call the primary and dual, respectively, $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances, which serve as lower bounds on relative generalized Hamming weights of nested primary and dual, respectively, one-point codes under similar condition. A unified method for establishing generic formulas for $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances of general numerical semigroups is developed and for a large variety of numerical semigroups generated by two elements, closed formula expressions are established complementing Delgado et al.s work [8] on the $m$th Feng-Rao distances of such semigroups. Finally, for non-singular $C_{ab}$ curves, we develop a method to estimate the $m$th relative generalized Hamming weight of dual and primary one-point codes in those regions where the generic methods may fail to provide best possible results.</p></details> |  |

These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |
| **[Cryptanalysis and Repair of "Decentralized Multi-authority ABE for NC$^1$ from BDH"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29224v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present a plaintext-recovery attack on both the DBDH-based construction and the CBDH-based variant of Datta, Komargodski, and Waters (Journal of Cryptology, 2023). The attack succeeds with probability one in the claimed static security model using one corrupt authority and two users, each unauthorized even when granted the corrupt authority's attribute. The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Our version of the proof gives $ω_\mathbb{F}\leq9/4$ over every field $\mathbb{F}$. We also treat the rectangular matrix multiplication exponent $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. While its mortality impacts are well recognised, the interplay between heat, air pollution, and socioeconomic vulnerability remains insufficiently understood. We quantified the associations of extreme maximum temperatures and heatwaves with daily mortality in Catalonia (2012-2022) and evaluated how air pollutants-particularly ozone, nitrogen dioxide and particulate matter-and socioeconomic factors confound or modify this relationship. We conducted a time-series ecological analysis across 379 Basic Health Areas during summer months, linking mortality with meteorological, air pollution and socioeconomic indicators. Hierarchical Bayesian zero-inflated negative binomial models were fitted with nested spatiotemporal random effects (Matérn SPDE field evolving over years; month nested within year; unstructured ABS frailty). Among 210.860 summer deaths in the complete environmental sample, extreme heat and heatwaves were associated with higher mortality after multi-pollutant and socioeconomic adjustment (e.g., extreme heat at lag 2: RR $1.105$, 95% CrI $1.086$-$1.125$; heatwave at lag 3: RR $1.142$, 95% CrI $1.116$-$1.167$; posterior Prob $\approx 1$). Ozone concentrations $\geq 100\,μ$g/m$^3$ were positively associated with mortality, and extreme heat$\times$high ozone interactions further elevated risk. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. Adaptation strategies should therefore combine heat-health warnings with air quality management and equity-oriented interventions.</p></details> |  |
| **[SoLiD26: A First Principles Solid-Liquid Interface Dataset for Machine-learned Interatomic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28013v2)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs) enable simulations of larger atomistic systems over longer timescales than traditional ab initio methods but require large, diverse training data. Existing datasets lack comprehensive sampling of solid-liquid interfaces, which exhibit complex phenomena such as solvent layering and surface reconstruction critical for energy and catalytic materials. Here, we present SoLiD26, a curated solid-liquid interface dataset of 15.4 million first-principles structures with up to 576 atoms and 15 chemical elements for training and evaluating MLIPs. The structures were compiled from density functional theory (DFT) calculations, with most configurations originating from ab initio molecular dynamics (AIMD) simulations. SoLiD26 includes aqueous coinage metal interfaces, electrode-electrolyte systems, and selected bulk reference structures, calculated with VASP using the PBE functional and D3 dispersion corrections. The application of SoLiD26 for training and evaluating MLIPs is demonstrated with a suite of MACE models on a simple training, validation and test split. This dataset provides a valuable resource for advancing MLIP development targeted at modelling solid-liquid interfaces in electrochemical and catalytic systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[A study of the $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances with a special emphasis on $C_{ab}$ curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26649v1)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In the seminal paper [13], Farrán and Munuera introduced a method to establish generic information on the $m$th Feng-Rao distance via a function on arbitrary numerical semigroups that serves as a lower bound on the generalized Hamming weights of dual one-point algebraic geometry codes whenever the semigroup is Weierstrass. In the present paper, we study what we shall call the primary and dual, respectively, $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances, which serve as lower bounds on relative generalized Hamming weights of nested primary and dual, respectively, one-point codes under similar condition. A unified method for establishing generic formulas for $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances of general numerical semigroups is developed and for a large variety of numerical semigroups generated by two elements, closed formula expressions are established complementing Delgado et al.s work [8] on the $m$th Feng-Rao distances of such semigroups. Finally, for non-singular $C_{ab}$ curves, we develop a method to estimate the $m$th relative generalized Hamming weight of dual and primary one-point codes in those regions where the generic methods may fail to provide best possible results.</p></details> |  |
| **[GOFusion: Real-Time Gaussian-Occupancy Submap Fusion for Fisher-Informed Autonomous Robot Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25261v1)** | 2026-10-18 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In this letter, we present GOFusion, a real-time visual mapping system for autonomous robot navigation that anchors Gaussian primitives to local occupancy maps, providing both spatial awareness for path planning and high-fidelity appearance that enables information-driven robot navigation. GOFusion builds 3D Gaussian maps from pose estimates and local occupancy maps obtained via visual-inertial SLAM and enables pose corrections to maintain both geometric and visual consistency. Furthermore, it restricts the 3D Gaussian Splatting optimization to active submaps to bound GPU memory usage. Building on the appearance-rich Gaussian map, we introduce a rasterizer-integrated Fisher approximation that quantifies per-primitive sensitivity online, serving as an information-driven heuristic for Gaussian pruning and next-best-view planning. We evaluate GOFusion on public datasets and in real-world drone flights. It not only outperforms several baselines in mapping quality, but also achieves real-time, high-fidelity and uncertainty-aware mapping at scale. Finally, we demonstrate fully autonomous drone exploration for Gaussian map refinement using the proposed Fisher-informed heuristic. Code is available at https://github.com/Bennnoo/GOFusion. We provide a supplementary video demonstration at https://youtu.be/pLQKbxQvb6I.</p></details> | 8 pages, 7 figures, 4 tables. Paper under review |

## Gaussian compression
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |

| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. | **[Cryptanalysis and Repair of "Decentralized Multi-authority ABE for NC$^1$ from BDH"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29224v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present a plaintext-recovery attack on both the DBDH-based construction and the CBDH-based variant of Datta, Komargodski, and Waters (Journal of Cryptology, 2023). The attack succeeds with probability one in the claimed static security model using one corrupt authority and two users, each unauthorized even when granted the corrupt authority's attribute. The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Our version of the proof gives $ω_\mathbb{F}\leq9/4$ over every field $\mathbb{F}$. We also treat the rectangular matrix multiplication exponent $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |
| **[A spatiotemporal Bayesian hierarchical model of heat-related mortality in Catalonia, Spain (2012--2022): The role of environmental and socioeconomic modifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.17148v3)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Extreme heat represents a growing threat to public health as climate change intensifies. While its mortality impacts are well recognised, the interplay between heat, air pollution, and socioeconomic vulnerability remains insufficiently understood. We quantified the associations of extreme maximum temperatures and heatwaves with daily mortality in Catalonia (2012-2022) and evaluated how air pollutants-particularly ozone, nitrogen dioxide and particulate matter-and socioeconomic factors confound or modify this relationship. We conducted a time-series ecological analysis across 379 Basic Health Areas during summer months, linking mortality with meteorological, air pollution and socioeconomic indicators. Hierarchical Bayesian zero-inflated negative binomial models were fitted with nested spatiotemporal random effects (Matérn SPDE field evolving over years; month nested within year; unstructured ABS frailty). Among 210.860 summer deaths in the complete environmental sample, extreme heat and heatwaves were associated with higher mortality after multi-pollutant and socioeconomic adjustment (e.g., extreme heat at lag 2: RR $1.105$, 95% CrI $1.086$-$1.125$; heatwave at lag 3: RR $1.142$, 95% CrI $1.116$-$1.167$; posterior Prob $\approx 1$). Ozone concentrations $\geq 100\,μ$g/m$^3$ were positively associated with mortality, and extreme heat$\times$high ozone interactions further elevated risk. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. Adaptation strategies should therefore combine heat-health warnings with air quality management and equity-oriented interventions.</p></details> |  |
| **[SoLiD26: A First Principles Solid-Liquid Interface Dataset for Machine-learned Interatomic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28013v2)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs) enable simulations of larger atomistic systems over longer timescales than traditional ab initio methods but require large, diverse training data. Existing datasets lack comprehensive sampling of solid-liquid interfaces, which exhibit complex phenomena such as solvent layering and surface reconstruction critical for energy and catalytic materials. Here, we present SoLiD26, a curated solid-liquid interface dataset of 15.4 million first-principles structures with up to 576 atoms and 15 chemical elements for training and evaluating MLIPs. The structures were compiled from density functional theory (DFT) calculations, with most configurations originating from ab initio molecular dynamics (AIMD) simulations. SoLiD26 includes aqueous coinage metal interfaces, electrode-electrolyte systems, and selected bulk reference structures, calculated with VASP using the PBE functional and D3 dispersion corrections. The application of SoLiD26 for training and evaluating MLIPs is demonstrated with a suite of MACE models on a simple training, validation and test split. This dataset provides a valuable resource for advancing MLIP development targeted at modelling solid-liquid interfaces in electrochemical and catalytic systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[A study of the $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances with a special emphasis on $C_{ab}$ curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26649v1)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In the seminal paper [13], Farrán and Munuera introduced a method to establish generic information on the $m$th Feng-Rao distance via a function on arbitrary numerical semigroups that serves as a lower bound on the generalized Hamming weights of dual one-point algebraic geometry codes whenever the semigroup is Weierstrass. In the present paper, we study what we shall call the primary and dual, respectively, $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances, which serve as lower bounds on relative generalized Hamming weights of nested primary and dual, respectively, one-point codes under similar condition. A unified method for establishing generic formulas for $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances of general numerical semigroups is developed and for a large variety of numerical semigroups generated by two elements, closed formula expressions are established complementing Delgado et al.s work [8] on the $m$th Feng-Rao distances of such semigroups. Finally, for non-singular $C_{ab}$ curves, we develop a method to estimate the $m$th relative generalized Hamming weight of dual and primary one-point codes in those regions where the generic methods may fail to provide best possible results.</p></details> |  |

## Gaussian uncertainty
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |

## feed-forward 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| **[Does VGGT Know What It Does Not Know?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35806v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models such as VGGT regress cameras and geometry in a single forward pass, but they report no uncertainty for the camera poses they predict: an incorrect relative pose looks no different from a correct one. We ask whether the frozen model nonetheless knows when its pose is wrong. On HardMatch, a benchmark of 1,000 deliberately difficult image pairs on which VGGT fails on 35% of pairs, a linear probe on VGGT's frozen features predicts pose failure on held-out categories with ROC-AUC 93.5%, compared with 79.7% for VGGT's own point-map confidence and 77.7% for a classifier trained on all of VGGT's outputs. The probe catches failures VGGT is confident about, lets a downstream system abstain on the pairs it distrusts, and transfers without retraining to rotation failures on PhotoTourism and ScanNet-1500, beyond what co-visibility explains. This knowledge is absent from the per-image encoder: it is computed partway through the layers that fuse the two views, and the same holds for a second model, $π^3$. In essence, we find that VGGT knows more about its own failures than it reports.</p></details> | 9 pages, 6 figures, 7 tables |
| **[CoMo3R-SLAM: Collaborative Monocular Dense SLAM with Learned 3D Reconstruction Priors for Outdoor Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30488v3)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Outdoor robot teams need a shared dense map despite limited overlap, independent reference frames, and uncertain monocular scale. Collaborative dense SLAM systems typically resolve this with depth sensors, which add payload, power, and calibration cost. We present CoMo3R-SLAM, a collaborative monocular dense SLAM system that places learned feed-forward 3D reconstruction priors at the center of the multi-agent problem: their dense pointmaps anchor scale across agents and supply correspondences strong enough to verify inter-agent links geometrically. Each agent tracks and fuses its own keyframes from a single RGB stream, while a coordinator retrieves cross-agent keyframes over the prior's encoder features, verifies them by bidirectional dense pointmap matching, synchronizes the independent similarity gauges in closed form, and refines every keyframe in one unified multi-agent sim(3) graph. Finally, a pose-depth alternation over geometry-aware segments lets inter-agent observations constrain dense structure as well as trajectories. Requiring neither measured depth nor supplied intrinsics, CoMo3R-SLAM attains the lowest trajectory error on three of four Tanks and Temples scenes, and competitive accuracy on Waymo driving sequences, while running at approximately 6-8 FPS on RTX 3080 Ti. A long-horizon traversal, independently captured day and night streams, and teams of up to four agents further map its operating range.</p></details> | Code and project website: https://como3r-slam.github.io |

| **[GeoLift: Grounding Video Spatial Reasoning in Explicit 3D Maps](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.33558v1)** | 2026-10-24 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video spatial reasoning requires multimodal large language models (MLLMs) to understand how objects are arranged and related across viewpoints. While these models can recognize objects in individual frames, integrating these observations to solve complex spatial tasks remains challenging. We examine route planning as a representative task and observe that existing models become substantially less accurate as the number of required turns increases, with several models achieving accuracy close to random guessing on routes requiring three or more turns. This finding suggests that recognizing landmarks alone is insufficient: models must also connect observations across viewpoints and track how landmarks are arranged in a shared space. Motivated by this observation, we introduce GeoLift, a training-free framework that uses feed-forward 3D reconstruction to map objects detected in individual frames into a shared metric 3D scene. GeoLift combines a frozen MLLM for 2D grounding with pretrained geometry models for scene reconstruction. It then uses shared geometric regions, called superpoints, to associate observations of the same object across views without a learned matcher. The reconstructed 3D map enables explicit computation of spatial relations and multistep routes, and provides geometric evidence for estimating distances and sizes. With all model weights frozen, GeoLift improves Qwen3-VL-4B's average score on VSI-Bench from 55.4% to 64.1%, including an increase in route-planning accuracy from 30.9% to 53.6%, and achieves 59.8% accuracy on MindCube. These results show that connecting frame-level object grounding through a shared 3D map can improve complex spatial reasoning without task-specific training.</p></details> | 11 pages, 3 figures, 4 tables. Xinglin Li and TingTing Long contributed equally. Jin Xie is the corresponding author |
| **[PixGR: Pixel-Space Generative Decoding for Fast Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31908v1)** | 2026-10-22 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present PixGR, an efficient framework that integrates lightweight pixel-space generative modeling into feed-forward 3D Gaussian reconstruction to model scene uncertainty. Context observations are encoded once into a scene latent representation, which conditions generative decoding from noisy target views to produce a shared 3D Gaussian representation. This avoids VAE-based latent compression and error propagation inherent in previous two-stage pipelines. To preserve feed-forward efficiency, PixGR caches the scene representation and performs iterative inference using a lightweight DiT decoder. Experiments demonstrate that PixGR outperforms regression-based approaches in challenging sparse-view settings, particularly with widely spaced observations and view extrapolation. Meanwhile, it maintains sub-second inference and remains competitive with substantially heavier pretrained generative models. Our project page is available at https://ranrhuang.github.io/pixgr/.</p></details> | Project page: https://ranrhuang.github.io/pixgr/ |

We conducted a time-series ecological analysis across 379 Basic Health Areas during summer months, linking mortality with meteorological, air pollution and socioeconomic indicators. Hierarchical Bayesian zero-inflated negative binomial models were fitted with nested spatiotemporal random effects (Matérn SPDE field evolving over years; month nested within year; unstructured ABS frailty). Among 210.860 summer deaths in the complete environmental sample, extreme heat and heatwaves were associated with higher mortality after multi-pollutant and socioeconomic adjustment (e.g., extreme heat at lag 2: RR $1.105$, 95% CrI $1.086$-$1.125$; heatwave at lag 3: RR $1.142$, 95% CrI $1.116$-$1.167$; posterior Prob $\approx 1$). Ozone concentrations $\geq 100\,μ$g/m$^3$ were positively associated with mortality, and extreme heat$\times$high ozone interactions further elevated risk. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. Adaptation strategies should therefore combine heat-health warnings with air quality management and equity-oriented interventions.</p></details> |  |
| **[SoLiD26: A First Principles Solid-Liquid Interface Dataset for Machine-learned Interatomic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.28013v2)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Machine-learned interatomic potentials (MLIPs) enable simulations of larger atomistic systems over longer timescales than traditional ab initio methods but require large, diverse training data. Existing datasets lack comprehensive sampling of solid-liquid interfaces, which exhibit complex phenomena such as solvent layering and surface reconstruction critical for energy and catalytic materials. Here, we present SoLiD26, a curated solid-liquid interface dataset of 15.4 million first-principles structures with up to 576 atoms and 15 chemical elements for training and evaluating MLIPs. The structures were compiled from density functional theory (DFT) calculations, with most configurations originating from ab initio molecular dynamics (AIMD) simulations. SoLiD26 includes aqueous coinage metal interfaces, electrode-electrolyte systems, and selected bulk reference structures, calculated with VASP using the PBE functional and D3 dispersion corrections. The application of SoLiD26 for training and evaluating MLIPs is demonstrated with a suite of MACE models on a simple training, validation and test split. This dataset provides a valuable resource for advancing MLIP development targeted at modelling solid-liquid interfaces in electrochemical and catalytic systems.</p></details> |  |
| **[Not All Tasks Quantize Equally: Fisher-Guided Quantization for Visual Geometry Transformer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15828v4)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models, represented by Visual Geometry Grounded Transformer (VGGT), jointly predict multiple visual geometry tasks such as depth estimation, camera pose prediction, and point cloud reconstruction in a single forward pass. They have been widely adopted in 3D vision applications, but their billion-scale parameters bring substantial memory and computation overhead, posing challenges for on-device deployment. Post-Training Quantization (PTQ) is an effective technique to reduce this overhead. Existing PTQ methods for feed-forward 3D models mainly focus on handling heavy-tailed activation distributions and constructing diverse calibration datasets. However, we observe that feed-forward 3D models predict multiple geometric attributes through a shared backbone, where different transformer blocks and hidden channels contribute distinctly to each task, resulting in substantially different sensitivities to quantization errors across tasks, blocks, and channels. Consequently, treating all tasks equally over-emphasizes insensitive tasks and causes significant accuracy loss on the sensitive ones. To address this issue, we propose Fisher-Guided Quantization (FGQ) for feed-forward 3D reconstruction models. Specifically, FGQ uses the diagonal Fisher information matrix to quantify the different sensitivities across tasks, blocks, and channels, and incorporates these sensitivities into the Learnable Affine Transformation during calibration to better preserve the channels and blocks most critical to each task. Extensive experiments across camera pose estimation, point map reconstruction, and depth estimation show that FGQ consistently outperforms state-of-the-art quantization baselines on VGGT, achieving up to 39% relative improvement under the 4-bit quantization. Code is available at https://github.com/ypzhng/FGQ.</p></details> | Accepted by NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[Latent Bridge: From Video Latents to 3D Geometry via Feature Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28521v1)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Video generation and feed-forward 3D reconstruction typically use separate representations, requiring RGB decoding and visual re-encoding to recover geometry from generated latents. We propose Latent Bridge, which connects video generative latent spaces with geometric feature spaces to directly predict 3D geometry from video latents. We develop representation-specific bridges for Wan2.1 and V-RAE, enabling feed-forward prediction of cameras, depth, and 3D point clouds without the intermediate RGB pathway. Each bridge is trained on RGB videos through feature distillation, while the video encoder and geometry model remain frozen. Training requires neither ground-truth geometry nor execution of the downstream geometric aggregator or prediction heads. At inference, the same video latents support both RGB decoding and geometry prediction. The same trained bridge can be reused for image-to-video and text-to-video generation within the same video latent space, without bridge retraining. Experiments on Co3Dv2 and WildRGB-D demonstrate competitive geometric reconstruction quality, efficient end-to-end generation and reconstruction, and faster latent-to-geometry decoding, highlighting latent alignment as a practical and reusable interface between video generation and 3D reconstruction. Our code is available at: https://github.com/hustvl/Latent-Bridge.</p></details> | Project page:https://shuaizhang7.github.io/latent-bridge-page/?case=car Code: https://github.com/hustvl/Latent-Bridge |
| **[PURE: Refining Feed-forward 3D Reconstruction with Unlabeled Post-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14698v2)** | 2026-10-20 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models have achieved remarkable progress through large-scale training and strong learned priors. However, they remain fragile when applied to dynamic in-the-wild videos, where non-rigid motion, occlusions, and illumination changes are prevalent. A key limitation lies in their reliance on expensive depth and camera pose annotations, which hinders scaling to diverse real-world dynamic data and restricts generalization. To address this issue, we propose \textbf{PURE}, an unlabeled post-training refinement framework that improves depth and camera estimation without requiring ground-truth supervision. Instead of relying on annotations, PURE elegantly distills robust structural knowledge from state-of-the-art 2D priors into 4D feed-forward architectures. PURE leverages relative depth constraints, affine-invariant geometry distillation, and pose refinement based on feature correspondences to stabilize geometric predictions under challenging dynamic conditions. Extensive experiments on both synthetic and real-world dynamic benchmarks demonstrate that PURE delivers consistent improvements across multiple state-of-the-art architectures. Furthermore, our data-efficient paradigm effectively mitigates domain shifts, successfully unlocking the architectural limits of these base models in challenging, in-the-wild dynamic scenes.</p></details> | Accepted by ACMMM 2026. Project Page: https://funnyguy525.github.io/PURE-Page/ Github: https://github.com/jiah-cloud/PURE |
| **[A study of the $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances with a special emphasis on $C_{ab}$ curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.26649v1)** | 2026-10-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In the seminal paper [13], Farrán and Munuera introduced a method to establish generic information on the $m$th Feng-Rao distance via a function on arbitrary numerical semigroups that serves as a lower bound on the generalized Hamming weights of dual one-point algebraic geometry codes whenever the semigroup is Weierstrass. In the present paper, we study what we shall call the primary and dual, respectively, $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances, which serve as lower bounds on relative generalized Hamming weights of nested primary and dual, respectively, one-point codes under similar condition. A unified method for establishing generic formulas for $m$th relative Feng-Rao distances of general numerical semigroups is developed and for a large variety of numerical semigroups generated by two elements, closed formula expressions are established complementing Delgado et al.s work [8] on the $m$th Feng-Rao distances of such semigroups. Finally, for non-singular $C_{ab}$ curves, we develop a method to estimate the $m$th relative generalized Hamming weight of dual and primary one-point codes in those regions where the generic methods may fail to provide best possible results.</p></details> |  |

## feed-forward Gaussian Splatting
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[TRUCO: A Scalable Lock-Free Algorithm for Parallel Contour Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.37297v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Transforming binary masks into geometric contours remains a critical step in many computer vision pipelines, particularly for post-processing high-resolution segmentation outputs. Yet, standard algorithms such as the Suzuki-Abe method are limited by their sequential nature and the overhead of building topological hierarchies. As modern applications transition to high-resolution imaging, there is a critical need for contour extraction that leverages multi-core CPU architectures without the complexity of tile stitching or heavy synchronization. This paper presents TRUCO (Threaded Raster Unrestricted Contour Ownership), a lock-free parallel algorithm designed for high-performance geometric boundary extraction. TRUCO introduces a start-point ownership rule, combined with a speculative tracing model, in which threads trace contours downward into adjacent regions and use an upward-termination logic to ensure each contour is processed exactly once. Experimental results on synthetic and real-world datasets, conducted on both consumer and server-grade CPUs, demonstrate that TRUCO significantly outperforms existing baselines. The method achieves speedups of over an order of magnitude compared to the industry standard while maintaining robust scalability on high-resolution scenes across diverse hardware architectures.</p></details> |  |
| **[GeoGSplat: Geometry-Grounded Non-Pixel-Aligned Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36760v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward Gaussian Splatting enables efficient novel view synthesis, but pixel-aligned prediction makes the primitive count grow with image resolution and view count, leading to substantial redundancy and increased computational cost. Non-pixel-aligned methods address this limitation by predicting a fixed number of Gaussians. Without explicit ray and depth constraints, these methods yield misplaced Gaussian centers, causing rendering artifacts under large viewpoint changes. To address this issue, we introduce GeoGSplat, a geometry-grounded feed-forward model that explicitly incorporates geometric priors into the non-pixel-aligned framework. A dual-branch encoder constructs Anchors and Triplane to capture coarse 3D structure and fine-grained scene features, respectively. Subsequently, a hierarchical decoder progressively decodes dense 3D points and Gaussian primitives, enabling 3D reconstruction and novel view synthesis. GeoGSplat surpasses both state-of-the-art pixel-aligned and non-pixel-aligned Gaussian Splatting methods on DTU and NRGBD in terms of novel view synthesis and reconstruction. The advantage of our geometry-grounded paradigm further increases as target viewpoints move farther from the input cameras, demonstrating stronger generalization to large viewpoint changes. Project Page: https://comedy0913.github.io/projects/geogsplat</p></details> |  |
| **[4D Reconstruction with Events &amp; RGB](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36610v1)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic 3D scenes over time (4D reconstruction) from a single moving camera is central to robotics and AR/VR applications, yet state-of the-art feed-forward methods rely solely on RGB frames and therefore inherit the failure modes of conventional cameras, including motion blur and temporal aliasing under fast or non-uniform camera and object motion. Event cameras report per-pixel brightness changes asynchronously at microsecond resolution, offering a complementary signal that is largely immune to these artifacts, but whether this modality can be incorporated into existing RGB based 4D reconstruction pipelines without a full architectural redesign remains an open question. In this preliminary study, we investigate a minimal extension: we augment Monst3R, an existing RGB-only 4D reconstruction model, with an additional event-camera input branch, leaving the rest of the architecture unchanged. Across 3 benchmark datasets, this simple RGB-event fusion reduces Abs Rel by 3.4% on average relative to the RGB-only Monst3R baseline, and a sensitivity analysis shows that the improvement grows with the magnitude of camera and object motion, with the largest gains observed under high object motion conditions where RGB-only reconstruction degrades most. These early results suggest that even a lightweight, non-invasive fusion of event data can meaningfully improve 4D reconstruction robustness, motivating a more thorough architectural investigation in future work.</p></details> | Accepted at NeVi 2026 workshop at the 19th European Conference on Computer Vision (ECCV 2026) |
| **[Cryptanalysis and Repair of "Decentralized Multi-authority ABE for NC$^1$ from BDH"](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.29224v2)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present a plaintext-recovery attack on both the DBDH-based construction and the CBDH-based variant of Datta, Komargodski, and Waters (Journal of Cryptology, 2023). The attack succeeds with probability one in the claimed static security model using one corrupt authority and two users, each unauthorized even when granted the corrupt authority's attribute. The flaw is an invalid extension of the LSSS matrix-normalization lemma originating with Rouselakis and Waters (FC,~2015): preserving complete-share distributions does not preserve coordinate-wise ciphertext masks, which a corrupt authority can expose to remove identity binding from other users' shares. For both variants, replacing these masks with independent full linear sharings of zero preserves correctness and restores ciphertext-distribution invariance. We prove static security of both repaired variants in the random oracle model under their respective BDH assumptions, using the original hardcore-bit convention for the CBDH variant. Both proofs allow adaptive identity-hash queries. The modification retains the original matrix-width and row-labeling restrictions, keys, decryption, and ciphertext size, requiring only additional encryption randomness and finite-field arithmetic. We also attack both multi-authority attribute-based inner-product functional encryption constructions of Datta and Pal (PKC, 2023) and establish correctness and matrix-substitution invariance of the same repair. We prove static security of the repaired small-universe construction under asymmetric DBDH. For the large-universe construction, we identify a missing source-group encoding in the original reduction and give a complete static security proof under an augmented $L$-DBDH assumption, whose generic bilinear group hardness we establish.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multiplying matrices via polynomials: exposition and barriers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30054v1)** | 2026-10-21 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We give an exposition of OpenAI's recent proof that the exponent $ω_\mathbb{C}$ of matrix multiplication over the complex numbers satisfies $ω_\mathbb{C}\leq9/4$. Our version of the proof gives $ω_\mathbb{F}\leq9/4$ over every field $\mathbb{F}$. We also treat the rectangular matrix multiplication exponent $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$. Here we have $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)=2$ for $0\leq k\leq1/2$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(1,k,1)\leq1+k+1/(4k)$ for $k\geq1/2$, and, for real numbers $a\geq b\geq c\geq0$, $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)=a+b$ if $c(a+b)\leq ab$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)\leq(a+b+c)/2+(ab/c+ac/b+bc/a)/4$ otherwise. Our exposition is in the language of degeneration inequalities among polynomial multiplication tensors and Strassen's asymptotic spectrum duality. We moreover prove an operational barrier (which can be thought of as coming from an operational asymptotic spectrum) showing that the current analysis of all these upper bounds on $ω_\mathbb{F}$ and $ω_\mathbb{F}(a,b,c)$ is optimal and that in fact the current degeneration ingredients cannot improve the bounds.</p></details> |  |

Ozone concentrations $\geq 100\,μ$g/m$^3$ were positively associated with mortality, and extreme heat$\times$high ozone interactions further elevated risk. Areas with higher income inequality and older population structures also showed elevated mortality. These findings indicate that heat indicators raise summer mortality in Catalonia even after environmental and socioeconomic adjustment, while compound ozone exposure and contextual vulnerability further shape risk. Adaptation strategies should therefore combine heat-health warnings with air quality management and equity-oriented interventions.</p></details> |  |
